#### Module `tokens` (app/actions/tokens.ts)

`TokenScope = "upload" | "read" | "write" | "full"`. The grants per scope:

- `upload`: identify, upload
- `read`: identify, read
- `write`: identify, upload, read, write
- `full`: everything above plus admin

`isNarrowing(cur, next)` is true when `next !== cur` and every grant of `next` is also a grant of `cur`.

##### tokens.createUploadToken
- Signature: `createUploadToken(name: string, scope?: string): Promise<{ token: string; scope: TokenScope }>`
- Auth: requireUser.
- Does:
  1. `chosen = isTokenScope(scope) ? scope : "upload"`. Absent or unknown values fall back to `upload`.
  2. `name?.trim() || "ShareX"`. It is **not cut**: above 120 chars the varchar column raises a DB error.
  3. `createApiToken`: `token = randomBytes(24)` base64url (32 chars); `INSERT api_tokens {user_id, name, scope, token_hash: sha256 hex, preview: last 6 chars}`.
- Returns: `{token, scope: chosen}` with the plaintext token, shown once.
- Errors: a DB error (for example a name that is too long) [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: `{"redirect":"/login"}` when there is no session (requireUser). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard/tokens`. Audit: none.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: tokens.ts:33 -> lib/api-token.ts:createApiToken, lib/api-scopes.ts:isTokenScope.

##### tokens.revokeUploadToken
- Signature: `revokeUploadToken(id: string): Promise<void>`
- Auth: requireUser.
- Does: `UPDATE api_tokens SET revoked_at = now WHERE id = $id AND user_id = me`. An already-revoked token is re-stamped; zero rows is silent.
- Returns: undefined.
- Errors: a non-uuid `id` gives a Postgres error [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: `{"redirect":"/login"}` when there is no session (requireUser). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard/tokens`. Audit: none.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: tokens.ts:44 -> lib/api-token.ts:revokeApiToken.

##### tokens.narrowTokenScope
- Signature: `narrowTokenScope(id: string, scope: string): Promise<{ ok: true; scope: TokenScope } | { ok: false; reason: string }>`
- Auth: requireUser.
- Does:
  1. An unknown `scope` returns `{ok:false, reason:"not-narrower"}`.
  2. `narrowApiTokenScope`:
     - `SELECT scope, revoked_at` for `(id, user_id = me)`; no row gives `not-found`.
     - A revoked token gives `revoked`.
     - An unknown stored scope, or a change that is not a narrowing, gives `not-narrower`.
     - `UPDATE api_tokens SET scope = next WHERE id AND user_id AND scope = <old> AND revoked_at IS NULL RETURNING scope`. No row (a race) gives `not-narrower`.
  3. On success, audit `api_token.narrowed {token: <api_tokens.id>, scope}`.
- Returns: `{ok:true, scope}` | `{ok:false, reason: "not-found" | "revoked" | "not-narrower"}`.
- Errors: a non-uuid `id` gives a Postgres error [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: `{"redirect":"/login"}` when there is no session (requireUser). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard/tokens` when ok.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: tokens.ts:63 -> lib/api-token.ts:narrowApiTokenScope, lib/api-scopes.ts:isNarrowing.

##### tokens.myTokens
- Signature: `myTokens(): Promise<ApiTokensRow[]>`, with `ApiTokensRow = { id: string; userId: string; name: string; scope: string; tokenHash: string; preview: string; lastUsedAt: Date | null; revokedAt: Date | null; createdAt: Date }`.
- Auth: requireUser.
- Does: `SELECT * FROM api_tokens WHERE user_id = me`. Revoked tokens are included, and there is **no ORDER BY**.
- Returns: full rows. The dates go out as `{"$date"}`.
- Errors: none of its own; the common failure modes in 7.2.6 apply.
- Redirect / notFound: `{"redirect":"/login"}` when there is no session (requireUser). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: none.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: tokens.ts:86 -> lib/api-token.ts:listApiTokens.
- Security: returns `tokenHash` (sha256 hex of each token) to the owner.

#### Module `unlock` (app/actions/unlock.ts)

##### unlock.verifyMediaPassword
- Signature: `verifyMediaPassword(mediaId: string, password: string, remember?: boolean): Promise<UnlockResult>`. The default is `remember = false`.
- Auth: public.
- Does:
  1. A falsy or non-uuid `mediaId` (case-insensitive check), or a `password` that is not a non-empty string, returns `{ok:false}` without counting. The ids are guarded.
  2. Rate limit: key `<clientIp>|media:<mediaId>` with the defaults (5 per 15 min). Over the limit returns `{ok:false, error:"rate-limited"}`.
  3. `SELECT uploads.id, uploads.password_hash, uploads.owner_id, uploads.password_version, user.default_password_version, user.default_password_hash FROM uploads LEFT JOIN "user" ON user.id = uploads.owner_id WHERE uploads.id = $id`. Expiry, trash and publish state are **not** consulted. No row, or no hash, returns `{ok:false}`.
  4. `verifyPassword` (format F). A mismatch returns `{ok:false}`.
  5. Success: reset the bucket. `onCurrentDefault = owner_id set AND owner default hash set AND upload.password_version != null AND password_version === owner default_password_version`.
- Returns:
  - `remember && onCurrentDefault`: `{ok:true, remembered:"owner"}`.
  - Otherwise `{ok:true, remembered: remember ? "file" : undefined}`. The key is present with value undefined when `remember` is false.
  - Failures: `{ok:false}` or `{ok:false, error:"rate-limited"}`.
- Errors: none of its own; the common failure modes in 7.2.6 apply.
- Redirect / notFound: none. Never notFound.
- Cookies on ok:
  - Always: `ml_unlock_<uploadId>=<exp>.<sig>` (parts `["media", id]`). The token TTL is **always 86400**. The cookie `Max-Age` is 2592000 when `remember`, else 86400. Attributes: `HttpOnly; Path=/; SameSite=lax`, plus `Secure` in production. As a result the "remember this file" cookie outlives its token, which stops verifying after 24 h.
  - When `remember && onCurrentDefault`: also `ml_dpw_<ownerId>=<exp>.<sig>` (parts `["owner", ownerId, "v<password_version>"]`), with TTL and `Max-Age` both 2592000 and the same attributes.
- Revalidate: none.
- Headers: X-Real-IP.
- Upstream: unlock.ts:41 -> lib/password.ts:checkRateLimit/verifyPassword/mintUnlockToken/mintOwnerUnlockToken/unlockCookieName/ownerUnlockCookieName/unlockCookieOptions/resetRateLimit (REMEMBER_TTL).

#### Module `uploads` (app/actions/uploads.ts)

Every action here is **session-or-throw**: no session throws `Error("Unauthorized")` [masked]. The actor is `{userId, email, isAdmin: role === "admin"}`. Admins reach every account's rows; everyone else only their own (lib/uploads.ts).

`getActionableUpload(actor, id)`:

- A non-string or non-uuid id, an unknown id, or a row the actor may not reach all throw `UploadOpError("not-found", "Not found")` [masked].
- Otherwise it returns the full row. Trashed rows are **included**.

`applyToUploads(actor, ids, patch)` (used by #85 and #92 to #94):

1. Re-reads the actionable rows; ids are deduplicated, and non-uuids dropped, up to 500.
2. `UPDATE uploads SET <patch>, updated_at = now WHERE id IN (rows) [AND owner_id = me for non-admins]`.
3. For each row **not** owned by the actor, ownerless rows included: audit `<action> {by, byEmail, uploadId, code, filename, ownerId, ...detail}`.

Unknown or foreign ids give a silent no-op.

`auditForeignUpload` writes nothing when `row.owner_id === actor`.

##### uploads.togglePublish
- Signature: `togglePublish(id: string): Promise<{ published: boolean }>`
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. `getActionableUpload(id)`.
  2. `published = !row.published`.
  3. `applyToUploads(..., {published})`.
  4. Foreign rows get audit `admin.upload.published {..., published}`.
- Returns: `{published}`.
- Errors: `"Unauthorized"`, `"Not found"` [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: none (no session is an error, not a redirect). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard` (when nothing threw).
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: uploads.ts:85 -> lib/uploads.ts:getActionableUpload/setPublishedFor.

##### uploads.trashUpload
- Signature: `trashUpload(id: string): Promise<void>`
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. `getActionableUpload(id)`.
  2. `softDeleteMedia(id)` = `UPDATE uploads SET deleted_at = now WHERE id AND deleted_at IS NULL`. `updated_at` is untouched. An already-trashed row is not re-stamped.
  3. `purgeDerived(id)`, which runs even when the row was already trashed.
  4. Foreign rows get audit `admin.upload.trashed`.
- Returns: undefined.
- Errors: `"Unauthorized"`, `"Not found"` [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: none (no session is an error, not a redirect). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard`.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: uploads.ts:95 -> lib/media/actions.ts:softDeleteMedia, lib/uploads.ts:auditForeignUpload.

##### uploads.restoreUpload
- Signature: `restoreUpload(id: string): Promise<void>`
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. `getActionableUpload(id)`.
  2. `UPDATE uploads SET deleted_at = NULL WHERE id`. There is no state check, so a live row gets a no-op write.
  3. Foreign rows get audit `admin.upload.restored`.
- Returns: undefined.
- Errors: `"Unauthorized"`, `"Not found"` [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: none (no session is an error, not a redirect). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard`, `/dashboard/trash`.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: uploads.ts:103 -> lib/media/actions.ts:restoreMedia.

##### uploads.deleteUploadForever
- Signature: `deleteUploadForever(id: string): Promise<void>`
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. `getActionableUpload(id)`. A row that is **not trashed** throws `Error("Not found")`.
  2. `deleteMediaHard(row)`: the storage object (errors ignored), the thumb, `derived/<id>/`, `DELETE FROM uploads WHERE id`, and `used_quota = GREATEST(0, used_quota - size)` for the owner.
  3. Foreign rows get audit `admin.upload.deleted {..., size}`.
- Returns: undefined.
- Errors: `"Unauthorized"`, `"Not found"` [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: none (no session is an error, not a redirect). Never notFound.

- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: uploads.ts:112 -> lib/media/actions.ts:deleteMediaHard.

##### uploads.trashSummary
- Signature: `trashSummary(ownerTag?: string): Promise<{ files: number; bytes: number }>`. The default is the caller's own tag.
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. Target tag: the trimmed `ownerTag` when it is a non-empty string, else `session.user.userCode ?? ""`.
  2. `trashOwnerId`. An empty tag, no user with exactly `user_code = tag` (aliases are **not** consulted), or another account's tag for a non-admin all throw `"Not found"`.
  3. `count(*)` and `coalesce(sum(size),0)` over `uploads WHERE owner_id = target AND deleted_at IS NOT NULL`.
- Returns: `{files, bytes}`.
- Errors: `"Unauthorized"`, `"Not found"` [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: none (no session is an error, not a redirect). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: none.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: uploads.ts:136 -> lib/uploads.ts:trashTotalsFor/trashOwnerId.

##### uploads.emptyTrash
- Signature: `emptyTrash(ownerTag?: string): Promise<{ deleted: number; bytes: number; failed: number }>`
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. The target is resolved as in #89.
  2. At most 500 rounds:
     - `SELECT id, owner_id, size, storage_key, thumb_key FROM uploads WHERE owner_id = target AND deleted_at IS NOT NULL [AND id NOT IN stuck] ORDER BY deleted_at ASC LIMIT 200`.
     - `deleteMediaBulk` (see the admin module). The quota is refunded.
     - Rows that storage refused are re-read into `stuck`.
  3. When the target is not the caller: audit `admin.trash.emptied {by, byEmail, ownerId, ownerTag, files, bytes, failed}`, once per call.
- Returns: `{deleted: files removed, bytes, failed: stuck.length}`.
- Errors: `"Unauthorized"`, `"Not found"` [masked].
- Redirect / notFound: none (no session is an error, not a redirect). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard`, `/dashboard/trash`.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: uploads.ts:158 -> lib/uploads.ts:emptyTrashFor, lib/media/actions.ts:deleteMediaBulk.

##### uploads.setVanity
- Signature: `setVanity(id: string, vanity: string): Promise<{ code: string }>`
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. `getActionableUpload(id)`.
  2. `code = slugify(vanity)` (a non-string becomes `""`; lowercase; runs of non `[a-z0-9]` become `-`; dashes trimmed). Empty throws.
  3. `isReservedTag(code)` (case-insensitive, list in lib/naming.ts) throws.
  4. Length above 64 throws.
  5. A `code` equal to the current code returns it unchanged, with no write and no audit.
  6. `SELECT id FROM uploads WHERE code = $code` over every account, trashed rows included. Another row with that code throws.
  7. `UPDATE uploads SET code, updated_at = now WHERE id [AND owner for non-admins]`. A `23505` violation throws `"That URL is already taken"`.
  8. Foreign rows get audit `admin.upload.link_changed {..., from: oldCode, to: code}`.
- Returns: `{code}`.
- Errors (masked):
  - `"Unauthorized"`, `"Not found"`
  - `"Invalid vanity"`
  - `"\"<code>\" is reserved by the app"`
  - `"Custom URL must be at most 64 characters"`
  - `"That URL is already taken"`
- Redirect / notFound: none (no session is an error, not a redirect). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard` (when nothing threw).
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: uploads.ts:171 -> lib/uploads.ts:setVanityFor, lib/ids.ts:slugify, lib/naming.ts:isReservedTag.

##### uploads.setUploadExpiry
- Signature: `setUploadExpiry(id: string, iso: string | null): Promise<void>`
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. `parseExpiry(iso)`: null, undefined or `""` give NULL. A Date is used as-is. Any other value goes through `new Date(value)`, with JavaScript parsing rules (the panel sends ISO 8601). An invalid date throws. Past dates are accepted.
  2. `applyToUploads(..., {expires_at})`.
  3. Foreign rows get audit `admin.upload.link_changed {..., expiresAt: ISO|null}`.
- Returns: undefined.
- Errors: `"Unauthorized"`, `"Invalid date"` [masked]. An unknown, foreign or non-uuid id is a **silent no-op**, not "Not found".
- Redirect / notFound: none (no session is an error, not a redirect). Never notFound.
- Revalidate: `/dashboard`.
- Cookies: none beyond the session maintenance in 7.2.3.
- Upstream: uploads.ts:187 -> lib/uploads.ts:setExpiryFor.

##### uploads.setUploadMaxViews
- Signature: `setUploadMaxViews(id: string, maxViews: number | null): Promise<void>`
- Auth: session-or-throw.
- Does:
  1. null or undefined gives NULL. A non-number or non-finite value throws. Otherwise `max(1, floor(n))`. There is no upper cap: values above the int4 maximum give a DB error.
  2. `applyToUploads(..., {max_views})`.
  3. Foreign rows get audit `admin.upload.link_changed {..., maxViews}`.
- Returns: undefined.

##### uploads.setUploadPassword

  3. `applyToUploads(..., {password_hash, password_version: NULL})`. `password_version` is always cleared.
  4. Foreign rows get audit `admin.upload.link_changed {..., passwordSet: boolean}`.
- Returns: undefined.

---

### 7.5 Open questions and upstream quirks to confirm

1. **Decided (7.1): yes.** **Cookie writes make Next refresh the page.** In Next 16 an action that sets or deletes any cookie triggers `x-action-revalidated`, and the client refreshes the current route even without `revalidatePath`. Should the envelope then carry `"revalidate": true`? The protocol as given only covers `revalidatePath`/`revalidateTag`. The question applies to:
   - #1 `updatePreferences` (theme or locale changed)
   - #58 `acceptInvitation`
   - #60 `signInWithIdentifier`
   - #75 `verifyShortlinkPassword`
   - #84 `verifyMediaPassword`
   - any action during which better-auth refreshed or expired the session cookie (7.2.3)
2. **Decided (7.1): `{"ok":{"$undefined":1}}`.** **Void results.** The proposal is `{"ok":{"$undefined":1}}`. The stub also works when `ok` is omitted. Pick one so differential tests are stable.
3. **Session maintenance on RPC.** Every non-public action can extend the session row and re-issue the session cookie, or expire a stale cookie (7.2.3). This belongs to the PORT.md 9 auth layer, but it must run on `/_rpc/*` exactly as it does on `/api/auth/get-session`.
4. **Decided (7.1): yes, 1 MiB, error envelope.** **Body limit.** Next refuses action bodies over 1 MiB before the action runs. The Rust `/_rpc` endpoint needs the same limit, answered with the error envelope. Without it, `createPaste` would accept up to 5,000,000 chars where upstream cannot.
5. **Non-uuid ids in SQL.** These upstream paths answer with the masked error envelope instead of a typed refusal: #6, #22, #40 to #43, #77, #78, #81, #82, and `createFolder` with `parentId: ""`. Keep this as is for 1:1?
6. **Upstream quirks a strict 1:1 port keeps.** None of them is in the PORT.md 12 fix list. Please confirm each:
   - a. #84: the "remember this file" cookie lives 30 days but carries a 24 h token.
   - b. #76 trims the short-link password; #75 verifies it untrimmed.
   - c. #73 writes `release_app` even when it then answers `not-public` (upload with an expiry, or without an owner).
   - d. #49 and #51: renaming to the same name, or moving within the same parent, changes the slug to `-2`.
   - e. #51 checks only the new parent's depth, so a subtree can end up deeper than 8 levels.
   - f. #67: a negative `expiresInDays` creates an already-expired paste; the returned URL ignores `skip_tag_in_url`; the default folder is not applied.
   - g. #42: an unknown `mode` replaces the file but is audited as `convert.copy`. #43: a non-uuid id aborts the batch after earlier commits, with no revalidate and no audit. A copy does not carry `duration_seconds`/`fps`.
   - h. #25 and #26: any role string reaches better-auth `setRole`, because no `roles` allow-list is configured.
   - i. #29 issues a password-setting link for any account, not only for pending invitations.
   - j. #8: the error "That pattern does not produce a usable name" can never fire.
   - k. #20 counts sessions after the admin plugin has filtered out impersonated ones, but deletes those too.
7. **Unordered queries.** #56 `listFolders` and #83 `myTokens` have no ORDER BY. Parity tests should compare their results as sets.
8. **In-memory rate limits.** They are per process (`lib/password.ts`). A multi-instance port behaves like several upstream processes. Note this only if the port will ever run more than one instance.
9. **Decided (4.4, 7.1): `digest` is part of the envelope.** **Error envelope shape.** PORT.md 7 shows the error envelope without `digest`, while this work order includes one. The SPA runtime accepts an optional `digest`.

Nothing else was left unclear from the code.

## 8. Better Auth endpoints the browser calls

`lib/auth-client.ts` (copied verbatim) is `createAuthClient({ plugins:
[adminClient(), twoFactorClient(), inferAdditionalFields<typeof auth>()] })`
from better-auth 1.6.13, with no `baseURL`: it calls `<page origin>/api/auth/*`
with `credentials: "include"` (same origin). The wire format of each endpoint
is Better Auth's own (PORT.md section 9): JSON bodies, JSON answers, errors as
`{ "code", "message" }` with an HTTP 4xx/5xx status, session cookies via
`Set-Cookie`. The components read `error.message`, `error.status` and the data
fields named below, so those must match Better Auth exactly.

| Browser call (component) | Request | Body | What the component reads |
|---|---|---|---|
| `signOut()` (components/app-shell.tsx, account menu) | `POST /api/auth/sign-out` | `{}` | nothing; then `router.push("/login")`, `router.refresh()` |
| `authClient.twoFactor.verifyTotp` (components/auth/two-factor-form.tsx, sign-in step 2) | `POST /api/auth/two-factor/verify-totp` | `{ "code": "123456", "trustDevice": boolean }` | `error`; on success `router.push(next)` + `refresh()` |
| `authClient.twoFactor.verifyBackupCode` (same form, backup code mode) | `POST /api/auth/two-factor/verify-backup-code` | `{ "code": "..." }` | `error` |
| `authClient.twoFactor.verifyTotp` (components/profile/SecuritySection.tsx, confirming setup) | `POST /api/auth/two-factor/verify-totp` | `{ "code": "123456" }` | `error.message` |
| `authClient.twoFactor.enable` (SecuritySection) | `POST /api/auth/two-factor/enable` | `{ "password": "..." }` | `data.totpURI`, `data.backupCodes`, `error.message` |
| `authClient.twoFactor.disable` (SecuritySection) | `POST /api/auth/two-factor/disable` | `{ "password": "..." }` | `error.message`; then `router.refresh()` |
| `authClient.changePassword` (SecuritySection) | `POST /api/auth/change-password` | `{ "currentPassword", "newPassword", "revokeOtherSessions": true }` | `error.message` |
| `authClient.requestPasswordReset` (components/auth/forgot-form.tsx) | `POST /api/auth/request-password-reset` | `{ "email", "redirectTo": "/reset" }` | `error.status === 400` only (anything else shows the neutral confirmation) |
| `authClient.resetPassword` (components/auth/reset-form.tsx) | `POST /api/auth/reset-password` | `{ "newPassword", "token" }` | `error` |
| `signUp.email` (components/auth/register-form.tsx) | `POST /api/auth/sign-up/email` | `{ "name", "email", "password" }` plus `"registrationKey"` when the page says a key is required | `data.token` (truthy = signed in), `error` (`code`/`message`, see the `hooks.before` gate in upstream lib/auth.ts: `SIGNUP_RATE_LIMITED` 429, `REGISTRATION_DISABLED` 403, `REGISTRATION_KEY_REQUIRED` / `REGISTRATION_KEY_INVALID` 400) |
| `authClient.getSession` (register-form, only when sign-up returned no token) | `GET /api/auth/get-session` | none | `data.session` |

`components/auth/auth-error.ts` turns an error into text: `error.message` when
present, else a translated fallback, so Better Auth's English messages show
as they do upstream.

Not upstream: while the optional Cloudflare Turnstile check is on (section 11),
`POST /api/auth/sign-in/email`, `/sign-up/email` and `/request-password-reset`
need the widget's token in the `x-captcha-response` header (the register and
forgot forms pass it as `fetchOptions.headers`). Refusals, before the endpoint
runs, use better-auth's captcha plugin's codes: no token `400
{"message":"Missing CAPTCHA response","code":"MISSING_RESPONSE"}`, refused by
Cloudflare `403 {"message":"Captcha verification failed","code":"VERIFICATION_FAILED"}`,
Cloudflare unreachable `503 {"message":"CAPTCHA service unavailable","code":"SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE"}`;
the forms show their own translated text for each (`turnstile.widget.*`).

Links the browser *follows* (documents, not fetches), which the Rust server
must answer like Better Auth does:

- the e-mail verification link (`GET /api/auth/verify-email?token=...&callbackURL=...`),
- the password-reset link (`GET /api/auth/reset-password/<token>?callbackURL=/reset`),
  which redirects to `/reset?token=<token>`, or to `/reset?error=INVALID_TOKEN`
  for a bad or expired token (the `/reset` page shows the "expired" card for any
  `error` parameter or a missing `token`).

Sign-in itself is not a client call: the login form uses the
`login-identifier.signInWithIdentifier` server action (section 7), and the
admin, invite and verification flows call Better Auth server-side from actions.

## 9. Other endpoints the browser calls

All of these are upstream route handlers or `server.ts` paths that the Rust
server implements anyway (PORT.md section 6); listed here because the SPA's
components call them directly:

| Endpoint | Caller | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| tus protocol on `/api/files` and `/api/files/<id>` (`POST`, `HEAD`, `PATCH`, `DELETE`, `OPTIONS`) | components/upload/uppy-client.ts, EncryptedUploader.tsx (`@uppy/tus`, `withCredentials`, chunk size `NEXT_PUBLIC_TUS_CHUNK_SIZE` at build time, else `DEFAULT_CHUNK_SIZE` in uppy-client.ts) | metadata key `folderId` (`components/upload/protocol.ts`: a folder id, `"root"`, or absent for the account default); the finished upload's link comes back in the `X-Media-Url` and `X-Media-Copy-Url` response headers |
| `POST /api/uploads/check` | components/upload/duplicate-preflight.ts | checksum preflight (`lib/upload/duplicate-check.ts` request/response types) |
| `POST /api/avatar`, `DELETE /api/avatar` | components/profile/AvatarSection.tsx | then `router.refresh()` |
| `POST /api/background`, `DELETE /api/background` | components/profile/BackgroundSection.tsx | then `router.refresh()` |
| `GET <convert URL>?check=1` | components/dashboard/use-convert-download.ts | the media action route answering whether a conversion is available |
| ranged `GET` of `/{tag}/{code}/raw...` | components/viewer/decrypt-transfer.ts (encrypted files) | `Range` header, `cache: "no-store"`; the first range starting at 0 is what counts the view |
| `<img>`, `<video>`, `<iframe>`, `<track>`, links: `/{tag}/{code}/raw[.ext][?w=..]`, `/download`, `/render?...`, `/timeline.vtt`, `/convert...`, `/avatar/{tag}`, `/bg/{tag}`, `/a/{tag}/{slug}/zip`, `/download/app` | viewer, dashboard, galleries | plain browser requests |
| `POST http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` | components/authorize/authorize-prompt.tsx | the desktop app's loopback listener (allowed by `/authorize`'s CSP only) |

## 10. What the SPA does on its own

What the server can rely on, and what it does not need to do:

- **Navigation.** `<Link>` and `useRouter().push/replace` navigate client-side
  when the target is a page of the SPA on the same origin. The route table is
  `src/contract/routes.ts`: `ROUTES` names the pages and `SERVER_PATHS` the route
  handlers and files the server answers itself, so that matching follows Next's
  precedence (`/s/promo` is the short-link handler, not a
  `[userCode]/[mediaCode]` page); `src/routes/tree.tsx` builds the router from
  both. Any other target is a document load, as Next falls back to a browser
  navigation (the fallbacks of section 4.2): another origin, a `SERVER_PATHS`
  path, a URL no route matches, a redirect to one of those, and a `/_page`
  request that fails or answers something that is none of the envelopes of 4.2.
  A `<Link>` also leaves modified clicks, `target` and `download` to the
  browser. `scroll: false` keeps the scroll position, otherwise a navigation
  scrolls to the top; back/forward restore it.
- **`router.refresh()`** fetches `/_page` for the current URL and re-renders with
  the new data, keeping component state (Next's semantics).
- **Loading UI.** `app/(app)/dashboard/loading.tsx` replaces the content while a
  navigation moves to a *different child segment* of `/dashboard`
  (`/dashboard` -> `/dashboard/folders`), not for query-only changes or deeper
  changes under the same child: that is when Next 16 creates a new Suspense
  boundary (its segment state keys ignore search params). Elsewhere the old page
  stays until the new one is ready.
- **Error UI.** `app/(app)/dashboard/error.tsx` for errors under `/dashboard`
  (the panel shell stays), `app/error.tsx` otherwise, `app/not-found.tsx` for
  `notFound`. Both error pages' "Try again" refetches the page. Server errors
  arrive as described in 4.4.
- **Redirect errors.** An action redirect navigates (push) and rejects the
  action's promise with Next's `NEXT_REDIRECT` error; a `redirect()` that escapes
  to the window is turned into a navigation, as Next's app router does.
- **Head and `<html>`.** The `meta` of every page and not-found payload (section
  4.2) is applied by `useHead` in `src/routes/root.tsx`: it sets `document.title`
  and the `description`, `application-name` and `robots` metas, creating a tag
  that is missing and removing one whose value is `null` or empty (so the media
  page drops its description and application name). The first payload leaves the
  document's other head tags alone; every later one (a navigation or a refresh)
  removes the Open Graph (`og:*`), Twitter (`twitter:*`), `canonical` and
  `alternate` tags of the first document, which describe that page only and
  exist for link-preview bots. Nothing else in `<head>` changes (the icon links
  stay). When the root layout data changes (after a language or skin change and
  a refresh) the SPA updates `lang`, `data-mode` and `data-theme` on `<html>`, as
  React does when the root layout's rendered attributes change; wherever a page
  renders `<ThemeApplier>` (the panel and the owner's public pages) its effect
  sets that page's skin and wallpaper after each navigation, and under
  `/dashboard` it writes `ml_skin`.
- **Translations.** Not bundled: they come with the page data.
  `layouts.root.messages` holds the 17 namespaces `i18n/request.ts` loads, for
  the request's locale (`server/messages/`), and `layouts.root.timeZone` the time
  zone; `src/pages/layout.tsx` hands them with `locale` to
  `NextIntlClientProvider`, as upstream's root layout did. The SPA keeps the
  dictionary it was sent (`src/runtime/messages.ts`) and names it on every
  `/_page` request with `X-Modlight-Messages: <locale>:<messagesHash>`; while that
  is still current the server answers `messages: null` (sections 4.1 and 5.7).
  The first document carries the dictionary in its inlined payload, and a
  language change brings the new locale's dictionary with the next page data.
- **Cookies the browser writes that the server reads**: `ml_mode`
  (components/theme-toggle.tsx), `ml_skin` (components/theme-applier.tsx, panel
  only), `ml_sidebar` (components/app-shell.tsx), `ml_dupes_snooze`
  (components/duplicates/DuplicateReminder.tsx). `ml_locale` is written only by
  the `account.updatePreferences` action.

## 11. Deviations from upstream

Components are byte-for-byte upstream (`upstream.lock.json`,
`npm run sync:check`), and so are the translations (apart from the Graphite
keys listed below): `server/messages/` is upstream's `messages/`, read and sent
by the server (section 5.7), not part of the SPA. What had to change, and why:

**Ported Server Components** (`src/overrides/components/`, each starts with a
header naming the change):

| File | Change | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `landing/{cta,features,hero,large-files,sharex,site-footer,site-nav}.tsx`, `dashboard/EmptyState.tsx` | `async` dropped; `getTranslations` (next-intl/server) -> `useTranslations` | async components cannot render in a browser |
| `profile/QuotaCard.tsx` | as above, and `fileCount/totalViews.toLocaleString()` -> `toLocaleString("en-US")` | upstream formats on the server, whose default locale is en-US (verified in the reference container), so every visitor saw "6,800"; the browser's default would differ |
| `tokens/AppCard.tsx` | as above; takes `release` as a prop instead of awaiting `currentRelease()` | server data; the tokens page payload carries it |
| `admin/ServerDisk.tsx` | `getLocale`/`getTranslations` -> hooks; takes `insights` as a prop instead of awaiting `diskInsights()` | server data; the admin page payload carries it |
| `viewer/MediaViewer.tsx` | `getTranslations`/`getFormatter`/`getLocale` -> hooks; `config` and `sandboxShape` resolve to browser versions | `lib/config` and `lib/media/render-ticket` are server modules; the browser config holds the public fields, the render-ticket mirror holds the pure `sandboxShape` |
| `duplicates/DuplicateReminderSlot.tsx` | renders the decision passed as `state` | the snooze check and the count are server work (`layouts["(app)"].duplicateReminder`) |

**Ported pages and layouts** (`src/pages/`): the JSX after `return` is upstream's,
except where a value upstream computed from server-only inputs is now a prop:
`(app)/layout.tsx` (all shell props), `admin/page.tsx` (`<ServerDisk insights>`),
`profile/page.tsx` (`folders`, `avatarUrl`, `backgroundUrl`), `links/page.tsx`
(`userCode`, `canUseRoot`), `tokens/page.tsx` (`<AppCard release>`, `user`),
`duplicates/page.tsx` (`userCode`), the public pages' `background`, and
`(auth)/authorize/page.tsx` (`session`; the signed-out redirect happens on the
server). `app/layout.tsx`'s `<html>`/`<body>` are the document template
(section 2.2). Pages whose query handling is presentation only read it in the
browser: `/2fa` and `/login` (`next`, through upstream's `safeNext`) and
`/reset` (`token`, `error`).

**Not upstream: optional Cloudflare Turnstile** (`server/src/turnstile.rs`,
SKELETON.md section 9 item 43). With no site key in `layouts.root.config`
nothing below happens and the forms behave exactly as upstream's.

- `components/auth/{login,register,forgot,invite}-form.tsx` are ports under
  `src/overrides/components/auth/` (the upstream copies are gone from
  `components/auth/`; `upstream.lock.json` tracks them as ports). Each is
  upstream's file plus lines marked `Turnstile`: `useCaptcha()`
  (`src/turnstile/widget.tsx`) renders the widget above the error banner, a
  submit without a token shows `turnstile.widget.required` and sends nothing,
  the token goes in `x-captcha-response` (`withActionHeaders()` from
  `src/runtime/rpc.ts` for the two actions, `fetchOptions.headers` for the two
  Better Auth calls), and the widget is reset after every attempt (a token
  verifies once). The refusals of section 7.4 (`captcha`,
  `captcha-unavailable`) and section 8 show as `turnstile.widget.*` texts; the
  forgot form does not show its "check your inbox" card for a refused check.
- The widget: Cloudflare's `api.js?render=explicit` is added to the document
  only when a site key is present, `size: "flexible"`, theme from next-themes'
  resolved light/dark mode, language from the locale.
- Admin -> Settings: the "Cloudflare Turnstile" card
  (`src/turnstile/TurnstileSettings.tsx`) under the platform settings, fed by
  the admin page's `turnstile` prop (section 6.20) and the server's
  `turnstile` actions (`loadTurnstileStatus`, `saveTurnstileSettings`,
  `clearTurnstileSettings`, `testTurnstileSecret`; stubs in
  `src/turnstile/actions.ts`, not in `src/rpc/actions/`, which is generated from
  upstream).
- Translations: `server/messages/<locale>/turnstile.json`, a namespace of its
  own, so upstream's files stay verbatim.

**Not upstream: per-account file encryption** (`server/src/vault/`,
`docs/ENCRYPTION.md`, SKELETON.md section 9 item 45). For an account without a
vault every payload is upstream's (the new props are absent), except the
Settings page's `encryption` prop, which is always sent.

- `src/encryption/`: the stubs of the server's `encryption` actions
  (`actions.ts`: `loadEncryptionStatus`, `enableEncryption`, `unlockMyFiles`,
  `lockMyFiles`, `changeFilePassword`, `disableEncryption`, `revokeVaultGrant`,
  `revokeAllVaultGrants`, `unlockOwnerFiles`; failures come back as
  `{ok: false, reason}`), the Settings card (`EncryptionSettings.tsx`), the
  unlock page (`VaultGate.tsx`), the library banner and the admin note
  (`VaultBanner.tsx`).
- Payload additions: `/dashboard/profile` `encryption: VaultStatus` (section
  6.18; `{state: "off"|"encrypting"|"on"|"decrypting", unlocked, progress:
  {done, total}, error, since, grants: [{id, kind: "owner"|"visitor"|"app",
  label, ip, createdAt, lastUsedAt, expiresAt, current}], minLength}`);
  `/dashboard` `vault?: {state, unlocked}`; the media page's fourth view
  `{view: "vault", mediaId}` (section 6.27; nothing else about the file);
  `/u/[userCode]` and `/a/[userCode]/[slug]` `vault?: {locked, tag}` with
  empty `items`/`tiles` while locked; `/dashboard/admin/users/[id]/files`
  `encryption?: string`; Admin -> Users rows `encryption?: string`.
- Ports: `components/admin/UserManager.tsx` (`src/overrides/`, one badge and
  one import path), and the pages above (lines marked "Not upstream").
- Translations: `server/messages/<locale>/encryption.json`, a namespace of its
  own (the 20th client namespace).

**Not upstream: the Graphite skin and layout** (the default skin).

- `lib/themes.ts` is a port (`src/overrides/lib/themes.ts`, mirrored by
  `server/src/themes.rs`): `graphite` leads `THEMES` and is `DEFAULT_THEME`, and
  the retired id `modern` (what sign-up writes for an account that never opened
  the picker) resolves to the default instead of `default` (Classic). Wherever
  this document says a value resolves to `"discord"` or that `modern` resolves
  to `"default"`, read `"graphite"`. The `user.theme` column default is
  `'graphite'` from migration 0014.
- The skin's tokens and its few `data-slot` rules are `src/styles/graphite.css`,
  loaded by `src/main.tsx` right after `app/globals.css` (which stays verbatim).
- `components/app-shell.tsx` is a port: the rail reads account card, navigation,
  then storage, Settings (the profile page, moved out of the Account section)
  and the app name with the collapse control; the header's account menu is
  phone-only. The rail carries `data-rail` for the skin's rules.
- `components/auth/brand-mark.tsx` and `components/brand/ModlightMark.tsx` are
  ports that draw the app icon (`src/assets/modlight-icon.png`) instead of the
  violet tile; `public/icon.svg`, `public/apple-icon.png` and
  `public/brand/{email-logo,audio-cover}.png` are rendered from the same icon.
- Translations: `profile.themes.graphite` and `nav.settings` are added to
  `server/messages/{en,pl}/` (the only lines there that are not upstream's).

**Infrastructure**:

- No server-rendered body. Upstream's first response already contains the
  page's HTML (under `/dashboard`, the shell and the loading skeleton first);
  the SPA's document carries the head, the `<html>` attributes, the theme script
  and the page data, and the body appears when the JS has loaded (one entry
  chunk plus the page's chunk, all cacheable). Link-preview bots read only the
  head, which is complete.
- Fonts: the same Geist woff2 files and `@font-face` rules as upstream's
  `next/font` output, served from `/assets/` with the stable class names
  `geist-sans` / `geist-mono` (Next's are hashed).
- A 501 box. While the Rust server has no loader for a page or one of its
  layouts (SKELETON.md section 6), `/_page` and the document answer 501
  `{"notImplemented": true, "route", "file", "layouts"}` (section 4.2) and the
  SPA renders a box naming the upstream `file` (`src/routes/not-implemented.tsx`)
  where the missing part would render: in place of the layout that is missing,
  else in the page's slot inside the layouts that loaded. Upstream has nothing
  like it. It shows in every build, because the Rust server always serves the
  production bundle, and goes away with the last 501.
- Layout data arrives with every navigation and the SPA uses the newest (Next
  keeps a shared layout until a refresh): the sidebar counts can be fresher.
- A server action's promise settles after the page data it revalidated has
  been fetched again (one extra round trip; Next ships both in one response).
- `next/dynamic({ ssr: false })` is `React.lazy` + `Suspense` with the same
  `loading` fallback; `useRouter().prefetch` is a no-op.
- `<ThemeApplier>`'s inline script does not run in a client render (it never
  runs on client navigations upstream either); the server-rendered document
  carries the final skin instead (section 2.2), and its effect does the rest.
- `server-only` and Node globals: `Buffer` and `process.env` are declared for
  the type checker only (`src/node-globals.d.ts`), because two shared upstream
  modules name them in server-only code paths; `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` are inlined at
  build time as Next does.

## 12. Open questions for the backend

Other sections refer to these items by number (for example 12.5): keep the
numbers and append new items at the end.

1. **`layouts.root.config`.** Section 5.7 asks for the five public configuration
   fields (`appName`, `embedBrand`, `appUrl`, `appDomains`, `shortUrl`) in the
   root layout data; the skeleton sends none. Until it does, the SPA falls back
   to origin-derived values (`Modlight` for the app name and the brand, the
   page's own origin for the three URLs; `src/overrides/lib/config.ts`), so a
   deployment that sets `APP_NAME`, `EMBED_BRAND`, `APP_URL`, `APP_DOMAINS` or
   `SHORTLINK_URL` would see the defaults wherever a page prints the app name or
   builds a link.
2. **The page's own skin and wallpaper in the document.** Section 2.2 lists, per
   page, the `data-theme` and wallpaper the document's `<html>` must carry
   before the first paint (upstream's `<ThemeApplier>` script did that). The
   skeleton's shell writes the root layout's `data-theme`
   (`resolveTheme(session theme ?? ml_skin)`) and no wallpaper, so until the SPA
   has loaded and `<ThemeApplier>`'s effect corrects it, the document has no
   wallpaper under `(app)` (and the wrong skin there when the stored theme is
   NULL), and the visitor's own skin and no wallpaper on an owner's public
   pages.
3. **`revalidate` on cookie writes.** Section 7.1 (decided in 7.5, item 1): a
   response that sets or deletes any cookie carries `"revalidate": true`,
   because Next 16's client refreshes the page then. The skeleton adds it only
   for `revalidatePath`/`revalidateTag` (`server/src/rpc/mod.rs`), so the
   actions listed in 7.5 item 1 (preferences, invitations, sign-in, the two
   unlock actions) and any action during which Better Auth refreshes or expires
   the session cookie would not make the SPA refetch the page.
4. **`/dashboard` redirects and `notFound`: 200 or 307/404.** Under `/dashboard`
   upstream answers `200` for a page's own `redirect()` or `notFound()` (the
   shell is already streamed behind `loading.tsx`; section 2.1 has the details,
   including the `<meta http-equiv="refresh">` of a redirect); the skeleton
   answers `307` and `404`, as everywhere else. The SPA handles both, so only
   the status line differs: keep the skeleton's, or answer `200` with the
   inlined redirect or not-found payload?
   **Decided (integration, 2026-10-20): `200` as upstream**, with the page's
   own title, `noindex` for `notFound()`, and the refresh tag inside
   `<noscript>` for a redirect (SKELETON.md 9.41).
5. **Malformed percent-escapes.** A path such as `/u/%E0%A4%A` is answered by
   the reference with `500` `text/plain` (section 5.3); the skeleton answers
   `400` `{"message": "Bad Request"}`, on the document and on `/_page`
   (SKELETON.md 9.1). The SPA loads any `/_page` answer that is not a page
   payload as a document (section 4.2), so either works: keep the `400`?
6. **The skeleton's head for `notFound`.** `server/src/pages/meta.rs`:
   `resolve(.., not_found = true)` always returns the root head, while inside
   the dashboard boundary upstream keeps the page's own title (`User files - A`
   for 6.21); OG/Twitter image URLs are emitted verbatim where Next
   WHATWG-normalises `og:image`/`twitter:image` (differs only for unusual
   `APP_URL`/`APP_DOMAINS` spellings).
7. **`notFound` `meta`.** Section 5.11 recommends upstream's server-rendered
   head (`{title: A, description: D, applicationName: A, robots: "noindex"}`)
   rather than the browser-side `generateMetadata` result, which for the media
   page would publish an unlisted file's name; confirm.
8. **Skins named like `Object.prototype` members.** `ml_skin` (or a stored
   theme) equal to an `Object.prototype` name (`toString`, `constructor`,
   `__proto__`...) makes upstream answer 500 (verified); reproduce, or resolve
   it to `discord`? The same plain-object lookups give absurd results elsewhere
   (`?size=constructor` on `/dashboard` reaches SQL; ext `constructor` in
   `MIME_BY_EXT`, `LONG_TYPES`, `EXT_CATEGORY`).
9. **`%` in a decoded segment.** A decoded segment containing `%` makes
   upstream's `generateMetadata` throw `URIError` (double decode, verified on
   `/x%25`); when the page itself renders (possible only with `%` in the
   decorative extension, e.g. `/tag/code.p%25ng`) the upstream outcome was not
   traced: recommend computing the metadata from the page's single decode.
10. **`preview.name` on `/invite/[token]`.** The port type sends `preview.name`
    to the browser, which upstream never did (only `email` reached
    `InviteForm`); keep or drop.
11. **Capped media.** Upstream computes the head and the view count
    concurrently, so the view that exhausts a cap may get the `Not found` head;
    section 6.27 recommends the pre-consume row.
12. **Preview bots and view-capped text.** Preview bots (not redirected) get the
    full media view of a view-capped text file, `textContent` included, without
    consuming a view; decide whether PORT.md security fix 1 covers this page.
13. **Date filters.** `/dashboard` `?after=`/`?before=` values that are not
    `YYYY-MM-DD` go through V8's lenient `Date` parser; the Rust server needs an
    equivalent for non-ISO strings or a documented subset.
14. **Ordering ties.** `ORDER BY` clauses without a tie-breaker (`listUploads`,
    short links, folder `sort_order, name`, gallery and album `created_at DESC`,
    which also picks the `og:image`) return ties in plan order; identical SQL is
    the closest match.
15. **Session refresh on page GETs.** Session refresh writes and cookie updates
    done by Better Auth `getSession` during a page GET belong to the Rust auth
    module (PORT.md section 9) and are not listed per page.
16. **Upstream returns secrets to their owner through two actions.**
    `shortlinks.listShortLinks()` and `tokens.myTokens()` return whole rows
    (`passwordHash`, `tokenHash`); the pages strip them, but the actions are
    callable endpoints. 1:1 keeps that; dropping the two columns from the RPC
    answers would break nothing in the SPA (no component reads them).
17. **Masked error messages.** Several components show `err.message` from a
    failed action (FolderTree, FolderBrowser, FileMenu, PasteEditor, SettingsForm,
    UserManager, OrphanFiles, Maintenance). Upstream in production shows React's
    masked text there (section 4.4); 1:1 means sending that text in `error.message`.
    A development mode that sends the real message (as `next dev` does) is fine.
18. **Upstream UI bug kept as is.** components/tokens/OneClickShareX.tsx passes a
    function for the plain argument `{fileName}` of `tokens.oneClick.downloadsHint`,
    so the file name never renders (React drops a function child; dev builds log
    an error). Fixing it means changing a component or the message.
19. **`/authorize` after an in-app sign-in.** `/authorize`'s wider CSP (loopback
    `connect-src`) only applies to a document loaded at `/authorize`. After the
    sign-in redirect the login form navigates there client-side (upstream does the
    same with Next's router), so the document is still `/login`'s and the loopback
    POST is blocked; the prompt then offers the manual copy. 1:1 keeps it; a
    document navigation for `/authorize` would fix it.
20. **Side effects of `GET /_page`.** As with upstream page loads, some page data
    has effects: the media page counts a view (and can burn a one-time link), the
    admin file browser writes an audit entry. Prefetching is off in the SPA, so
    only a real navigation triggers them.
21. **Dev server port.** `npm run dev` uses `127.0.0.1:5173` (PORT.md section 3
    has no row for it).
22. **Server-action quirks** (7.5, items 5 to 8): non-uuid ids answered with a
    masked database error instead of a typed refusal, eleven behaviours a strict
    1:1 port keeps (a 30-day cookie carrying a 24 h token, the short-link password
    trimmed on save but not on check, `publishAppRelease` saving before refusing,
    any role string accepted by `setRole`, `resendInvite` for any account, ...),
    two unordered queries, and per-process rate limits. Each needs a keep-or-fix
    decision; none is on PORT.md's security-fix list.
23. **`revealRegistrationKey` and `createAccount`** return the plaintext
    registration key and raw (unmasked) mail error text to administrators, as
    upstream does (7.4). 1:1 keeps both.
